January 1, 1994


To Sacred Harp Singers and Friends:


A new year is here, new minutes are out for your use for 1994. The
address of some new singers have been add, the deceased deleted. I know
some addresses are incorrect, but you failed to send the new ones to me. If
you have a change of address please sent it to me by August 1.
I cannot print poems that are used in the memorial lesson because of
copyright. Please write your own memorial lesson so it can be printed.
we had five singing discounted this year which makes a total of 76 singing
discountinued in the last ten years.
Lets encourge other people young and older to learn the Sacred Harp
Music. We have lost so many of our older singers, we are seeing our
classes become very small in most areas now. In order for the Sacred Harp
singing to grow we must have more people learn to singing this music.
Some of our singers now seen to thank it is a place to go for fun, are see
who can singing the fastest. This should not be the case, our forfather
went and our older singers go now to sing praises to our Lord. Look at the
Bible reference under the names of the song, see where the words of the
song are from. These older writter of Sacred Harp Music wrote the songs
of praise to be sung in reverence to our Lord. Lets carry the singing own
as our forfather did. If we singing so fast our vistors do not understand
the words or notes. I have had several people making this statement "We
cant unders stand what is being sung".
We still have some secretary who do not send the minutes in on time.
Please get your minutes to me on time so they may be in order. All 1993
December singing will be printed in front of 1994 minute. This must be
done if you receive your minutes in February. Continue to send $25.00 per
day and $12.50 for night singing. Make your checks to Sacred Harp
Singings.
Hope each of you have a good year in 1994.


Nora Parker, Secretary

Marion, Kentucky
January 23, 1993
The annual Four Note Singing was called to order at 9:30 a.m. by
William Paris. He introduced Judge Jon May who welcomed singers to
Marion. Mr Paris announced that both morning and afternoon session would
consist of about one hour's singing from each of two books, The Southern
Harmony and The Sacred Harp. Tim Reynolds distibuted a list of 150 songs
that are in both books. The chairman called the fist hour of singing to order
by demonstrating the pendulum for keeping time, as explained in the
introduction to The Southern Harmony. Knots in the string cause the
pendulum to sway at different speeds, setting the time for 2/4, 4/4, 6/8
and so on. Any disagreement about the tempo of a song could be settled by
the pendulum.
Mr Paris led song on page 66, "Pleasant Hill" from the Southern
Harmony, dedicating it to Pleasant Hill Church, which revised shape note
singing in 1978 and still uses this music as a congregation. Next he led
song on page 192b. Leaders were called and the Southern Harmony book
was used: Karen Isbell, Becky Browne and Jo Dell Albi, led 3, 7; Beth
Hoffman, 76, 16; Jo Dell Albi, 94, 104; Judy Hauff, 107, 115; Tim
Reynolds, 286, 322; Jim Page 50, 102; Bill Carlson, 252; Dave Ressler,
48, 306.
RECESS.
The class resumed singing with Tim Reynolds leadings a song from the
Sacred Harp on page 84. Leaders: Jim Page 272; Bill Carlson, 276; Dave
Ressler 66; William Paris, 36b, 89; Karen Isbell, 215, 29: Becky
Browne, 3873, 143; Beth Hoffman, 107, 148; Jo Dell Albi 180, 542;
Judy Hauff, 300, 87.
DISMISSED FOR LUNCH.
The afterrnoon session reconvened with William Paris leading songs on
pages 308 and 38 from the Southern Harmony. Leaders: Karen Isbell, 89b,
277; Becky Browne 40, 178; Beth Hoffman, 120, 276b; Jo Dell Albi, 92,
76; Jim Page, 89, 72; Bill Hunt, 103; Arnold Hunt, 71; Judy Hauff, 148,
151; Tim Reynold, 325, 177; Bill Carlson, 53; Dave Ressler, 64, 170.
RECESS.
Tim Reynolds called the class together leading songs on pages 178 and 45
from the Sacred Harp. Leaders: Dave Ressler, 209, 495; Bill Paris, 236;
23


Karen Isbell, 145b, 168; Becky Browne, 384, 547; Beth Hoffman, 385,
335; Jo Deil Albl, 497; Jim Page, 368, 352; Judy Hauff led song on pages
277 and 347 as the closing songs. The class was dismissed with praver
by William Paris.
Chairman, William Paris; Vice Chairman, Tim Reynolds; Secretary, Karen
Isbell.

OHIO STATE CONVENTION
Columbus, Ohio
February 20, 21, 1993
The first annual session of the Ohio State Sacred Harp Convention began
on the evening of Friday, February 19, 1993, with a singing school taught
by John Bealle. The first regular singing session was held on Saturday,
February 20 at Northminster Presbyterian Church, Columbus, Ohio. Fred
Todt called the class to order leading song on page 38 in recognition of the
snow blanketing the Coumbus Area. Steve Tossey led the morning prayer
A motion was made by Fred Todt to go into a business session. The
following slate of officers was nominated and elected: Chairman, Fred
Todt; Vice Chairmam Linda Coppock; Secretary, Eric Conrad; Treaurer,
Paul Foster. Arranging Committee, Eloise Clark, Michael Darby, Leslie
Scott; Memorial Committee, Bobbie Goodell; Sarah Breidenstein, Marcia
Johnson; Resolutions, John Bealle and Mike Hieber; Chaplains, Steve
Tossey and John Bayer. Leaders: Linda Coppock, 178; Eric Conrad, 107;
Paul Foster, 481; Eloise Clark, 455: Michael Darby, 131b; Leslie Scott,
53; Marcia Jonbson, 89; Mike Hieber, 276; John Bealle, 278; Bobbie
Goodell, 47; Steve Tossey, 198; John Bayer, 102; Sylvia Thomas, 551;
Dick Dunagan, 503; Nathan and Emmie Barford, 457; Kay Bieszczad, 354;
Virginia Cameron, 490.
RECESS.
Linda Coppock called the class to order leading songs on pages 128 and
34b. Leaders: Jeff Franklin, 64; Wendy Lee" Salem: (arranged by Richard
Popp); Jack Cummins, 28b; Connie Karduck, 456; Mathew Lind, 299;
Samuel Sommers, 85; Jim Page, 550; Emmie and Nathan Barford, 45;
Stephanie Hysmith, 153; Richard Popp, "Hickman“; Michele Rae Biery with
Brad Oglesby, 373; Faye Bresler, 268; Pauline Childers, 269; Ted Mercer

Johnson explained the tradition of the memorial lesson for singers new to

singer of Sacred Harp for many years who gave support and ecouragement
to Northern singers new to Sacred Harp music. Marcia invited Reba
Windom, Leonard's neice, to assist her in leading songs 101 and especially
for the basses, 373. In remembrance of James M. Windom, Sr., Marcia
Johnson led 358. John Bayer led in prayer.

98. The committes's made their reports at this time. Treasurer, Paul
Forster reported that the expenses of the Convention had been met by the
generous donations of those attending. The Arranging Committee reported
that 115 singings attended the convention with 100 singers registering on
Saturday and 15 additional singers registering on Sunday. A total of 64


34


different people led 170 songs with 52 leaders and 88 songs on Saturday
and 33 leaders and 82 songs on Sunday. The following nine states were
represented; Illinois, Indiana, Maryland (D.C. Area), Massachusetts,
Michigan, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Wisconsin, and West Virginia. The
Resolutions Committee presented the following report: We wish to thank all
of those who have contributed in so many ways to make this first Ohio
State Sacred Harp Convention a success. Fred Todt and the Columbus
singers have done an excellent job hosting the convention. We are
especially indebted to several people whose contribution have exceded the
call of duty. John Bayer for making the beautiful signs directing people to
our convention, Rev. Jaffrey Thompson for donating a pleasant facility for
the Saturday singing. Lorraine Bayer for cooking more than her share, and
Lorraine Sawyer and Lorraine Bayer for producing our first ever State
Convention dinner on the grounds. Finally we thank God for the privilege of
coming together in sweet fellowship to sing Sacred Harp. Resolutions
Committee, John Bealle and Mike Hieber. The class amended the resolution
to thank John Bealle for his vision in suggesting that this convention was
possible, for his guidance to the Ohio singers and for the labor he
contributed to make this convention a reality. The second annual Ohio State
Sacred Harp Convention will be hosted by the Concinnati singes on the third
Sunday and Saturday before in February, 1994. The officers, Fred Todt,
Linda Coppock, Eric Conrad and Paul Foster and several others led the class
in "Parting Hand" on page 62. John Bayer dismissed the class with
prayer.
Chairman, Fred Todt; Vice Chairman, Linda Coppock; Secretary, Eric
Conrad.

PACIFIC NORTHWEST CONVENTION
Portland, Oregon
February 27, 1993
The second annual session of the Pacific Northwest Sacred Harp Singing
Convention was held on Saturday, February 27, at the Bethlehem Lutheran
Church in Portland, Oregon. The convention was called to order at 9:30
a.m. by Karen Willard leading song on page 82. The morning prayer was led
by Kate Moore. Leaders: David Hough, (WA)) 47b, 49b; Anne Huckins
(WA)) 84, 47; John Kellermannn, 65, 64. The class then went into a
business session and officers were elected as follows: Chairman, Kate
Moore; Vice Chairman, Marie Brandis; Secretary, Karen Willard;
Arranging Committee, John Kellermann; Memorial Committee, Shelbie
Sheppard, Martha Johnson and B.M. Smith; Finance Committee, Kurt
Liebezeit; Resolutions Committee, Jack Lofton and Anne Reif; Locating
Committee, David Hough and Ken Hallock. Leaders: Kurt Liebezeit, 189,
180; Jack Lofton (WA) 344, 277; Karen Stingle, 184, 153; Bruce
Rowland, 454, 455; Marianne Costello (WA), 49, 63; Chris Thorman (CA),
269, 532; Cindy Willard (CCA) 85, 128; Jeff Sheppard (AL) 155, 352.
RECESS.
Marie Brandis called the class together leading song on page 146.
Leaders: Shelbie Sheppard, (AL) 327, 503; Susan Helf (WA) 479, 112;
Melanie Willard, 34b, 159; Carolyn Deacy (CA) 504, 299; B. M. Smith,
(GA) 497, 441; David Jensen "Shiloh“ 179; Martha Johnson, 266, 312b.
DISMISSED FOR LUNCH.
The afternoon session reconvened with Marie Brandis leading song on
page 117. Leaders: Ken Cofield (WA) 147, 361; Glenn Salisbury (WA) 581,
59; Karen Willard, 303, 464; Marie Brandis, 501b: Kate Moore, 324,
380; B.M. Smith, 276; Chris Thorman, 200, 300; Shelbie Sheppard, 368,
556; Jean Murphy, 268, 35. The Memorial lesson was held at this time.
36


Shelbie Sheppard explained what traditional Sacred Harp Singers do during
a memorial lesson and why. B. H. Smith led 101 and 28b for the following
deceased from Alabama: Zera Tolleson, Leonard Lacy, Leman Brown and
Mary Carter Johnson. John Kellermann led 209 for the deceased from the
Pacific Northwest: Joe Teague, Pat Cutler, Sidney Rust, Sammy Cahn, Don
Thompson, P.D. Larson, Karyn Dashbach, Herman Chyba, Randy Forrer,
Dorothy Rogers, Otis Thorman, Margarethe Stevens, and Kenneth Hurd of
CA. For the sick and shut-ins Martha Johnson led song on page 284 and
read the following names: Brian VanderPloeg, Ellie and Denny Stucke, and
Florence Varner. Martha Johnson ended the memorial lesson with prayer.
Leaders: Jack Lofton, 254, 183; Karen Stingle, 208, 228.
RECESS.
The class was called to order and the following leaders were called:
Susan Helf, 39, 191; David Jensen, 89, 500; David Hough, 163b, 69; Kurt
Liebezeit, 1876; Reed Schilbach, 143, 410; Anne Huckins, 107; Glenn
Salisbury, 185: Marie Brandis, 288. At this time reports were given:
The Finance Committee reported a surplus of funds after deducting all the
expenses of the 1993 convention, which will be used for the 1994
convention. The Resolutions Committee Thanked "the officers of 1992,
Karen Willard, Ann Huckins and David Hough; The 1993 officers; the hard
working crew in the kitchen: Heg Larson, Kate Moore, Marie Brandis, Lori
Hazelhorst, and Carla Kelley; the friendly faces at the registration table
Jane VanderPloeg and Carla Kelley: our childcare provider, Tammy
Burngardt; and the set up and clean up crew: Meg Larson, John Kellermann,
David Hough, and Ken Hallock. Kate Hoore merits special thanks as the
one-person locating committee, publicity maven, and general all-around
organizer for the 1993 convention. A very special thank you must also go
to Jeff and Shelbie Sheppard who came all the way from Glencoe, Alabama
to share their love and knowledge of Sacred Harp traditions with us. The
Location committee reported that a good site in Preston, Washington had
been located, but other sites in the city of Seattle, Washington were also
under consideration. It was voted that the 1994 Pacific Northwest
Convention would be held in the greater Metropolitan Area (considered to
include Preston) or Seattle, Washington. Singers came from the following
states: 53 from Oregon, 22 from Washington, 3 from California, 2,
Alabama and 1 from Georgia. Kate Moore and Marie Brandis led song on
page 62 as the closing song and all who wished took the Parting Hand. The
convention was dismissed with prayer by Kurt Liebezeit.
Chairman, Kate Moore; Vice Chairman, Marie Brandis; Secretary, Karen
Willard.

the Folk Eucharist in the St. Joseph Chapel of the Washington Cathedral.
Hugh NcGraw was invited to speak to the congregation about Sacred Harp.
Ella Wilcox opened the Sunday session leading song on page 171. Leaders:
Zena Tucker, 406; Carol Werba (NJ), 65; Connie Karduck, 335; Tom
Padwa, 56; John delRe, 56b; Pat Hilgard, 487; Karen Foley, 84; Joseph
Christopher 49b; John Currie, 359; Molly Sayvetz, 122; Mary Oruc
(TX), 66 Gavin Campbell, 347; Jean Seiler, 277; Carol Werba, 178; Eric
Bean, 181. A Few announcements were made. Leaders: Donna Abranams
474; Jerry Enright, 300; Judy Mincey, 31; Susan Mampre, 280; Beth
Hoffman, 547; Connie Karduck, 501b; Glen Wright, 368; Dottie Hurley,
480; Bob Hall, 297; Ginnie Ely, 428. The Memorial lesson was called by
Ella Wilcox, who turned the floor over to Amanda Denson. Howard Brady
read Psalm 103: 14-17 For he knoweth our frame; he remembereth that
we are dust. As for man, his days are as grass: as a flower of the field,
so he flourisheth. For the wind passeth over it, and it is gone; and the place
thereof shall know it no more. But the mercy of the Lord is from
everlasting to everlasting upon them that fear him, and his righteousness
unto childrens children." Amanda Denson then made remarks summarized
as follows: " When I was twelve years old, Uncle Pain Denson, Aunt Ruthy
(Ruth Denson Edwards). Aunt Annie (Ann Denson Aaron ) and I went to the
Chatlahoochee Convention in Georgia. The cream of the crop were there,
and we had a glorious time, but when we got home I said, Aunty Ruthy, it
takes so long when we sing for those dead people. She replied, Amanda,
you will grow to love this part best," and I have. It has become my
favorite part of the singing. All of those people sang for others all their
lives, and now we are singing for them, and some day our children will sing
for all of us. The cycle goes on and on. We are only a link in a chain -- a
golden chain. It makes me happy to sing today with members of the
Godsey, Lacy, Creel, McGraw and Beasley families, my own family and
other families long associated with Sacred Harp. However, those who have
gone on before us can outclass any voice in this room. It's not death--
period, but death--comma.
"You do not make any thing the foundation of your life that can be Laken
away from you. The people we remember today knew that, and Sacred
Harp was a large part of many of their lives. This is part of the reason for
a Sacred Harp heart. If you have never heard that term before, it means
goodness and kindness personified. It is what makes us like an extended
family, and it is what makes us endure from generation to generation. Hans
Christian Andersen said, "Every man's life is a fairy tale written by God's
fingers." We are all artists. We are each painting a picture--a picture of
life." In closing. Amanda Denson said, "It is Palm Sunday and for all
friends, loved ones, and Sacred Harp singers of all time who already have
the resurrected life, let's sing Easter Anthem." Then Hugh Mc Graw and
Amanda Denson led 236. Pat Higard read the names of the sick and
shut-ins: W. Smith (MD); Pansy McConnell (NM), John and Catherine
DeNys (IL), George and Pat Lippi (WI), Mother and Father Rudolph (ND),
53


Marjory Barks, (NY), Jerry and Fozell Sheppard (AL), Lonnie and Vivian
Rogers (GA), Laura Fargo (AL). Sarah Smith led 61 in their honor. The

memory of Hille W. delRe, Wayre Welch, Dr. Rick Ready, Nargo Sylvester
(All from VA). Dr. J. D. Broussard (MD). For the Northeast, Eric Bean led

deepening and developing what Amanda has called a "Sacred Harp heart".
We thank all the singers here and those afar who have nurtured us along the

al such perfect peace. She spoke of the words in the song by Judy Hauff

GARDEN STATE SACRED HARP SINGING CONVENTION
Montclair Friends Meetinghouse, Upper Montclair, New Jersey
May 14-15, 1993
The first Garden State Sacred Harp Singing Convention was called to
order the evening of Friday, May 14. George Seiler gave the opening
prayer and welcormed all present. He then introduced Amanda Denson,
presently of Tuscaloosa, Alabama, who joined him in leadinq 147. Durinq
the singing school that followed, Miss Denson who represents five
generations of Sacred Harp singers taught an appreciative class of 87
students the rudiments of Sacred Harp Singing. Thanks to her knowledge
and enthusiasm, all present rejoiced in singing a wide variety of songs,
from simple hymns to complex anthems. Dan Brittain was called upon to
lead one of his compositions, Cobb (313). Howard and Mandy Brady led
page 40, and Mandy joined her mother Amanda Denson, in leading 217.
The final song of the evening was Montclair, composed by Bruce Randall in
honor of the occasion. The class was dismissed after an expression of
gratitude and a closing prayer by George Seiler.
SATURDAY, MAY 15, 1993

The class reassembled with Petrina Pattie leading song on page 276.
Leaders: Lissa Blood, 290; Tom Terranova, 504; Julie Vea (WI), 266;
Fike Heiber (CH), 288. The convention was then turned over to the
Memorial Committee. Meg Parsons began with these thoughts: This is a
very special singing, and this is a very special weekend. This is Memorial
Day weekend, and think it's appropriate that on this day we remember the
members of our extended community- people who have died in the past
year, or who are sick and not able to be with us. When we read their
83


names, each of them holds a special meaning to each of us, because they
are a part of our community. As we read their names, and as we sing the
songs, we'd like to reflect on what those words mean- What the songs
mean to us as a community. In addition, as we sing the songs, as we do all
the time, I think, we remember people who are special to us, people who
have been influential in our lives- grandparents, parents, brothers,
sisters, spouses- whove gone on before us. On this Memorial Day
weekend, we think it's also appropriate that we remember people whose
names we don't know, people who have died in wars, men and women
who've given us the freedom to be here today; also people from around the
world who are now suffering in war and famine; People here in our
community, who are alone, afraid, sick, hungry. For those of us whove
either been born to the tradition of Sacred Harp, or who have found it
recently in their lives, we are very fortunate. We are a community. If we
are not together by location, as we are today, as we sing the songs at
home we think of each other. We comfort each other by singing our songs,
as we think of each other, and as we think of God. Helen Bryson then read
the names of the deceased: Virgina, Hilda delRe; Michigan, James Windom
Sr., Kirk Norton, Fred Buesser. Florida, Jane Rend. Alabama. Leonard
Lacy, Zera Tolleson, Mrs. Odell Cleveland, J.W. Busby and G. Grant.
Illinois, Robert Rokusek, Agnes Witt, Jeanine Kowalski, Samuel Freeman,
Indiana, Susie Coning. Wisconsin, Pat Collier, Father Luke Radke.
Pennsylvania, Lora Rill. Gerogia. Garfield Rainwater, Josie Bell Avery and
Gary DeLong. Freeman Wooten, who was asked to lead for the deceased,
spoke briefly before his lesson "It's an honor for me to be here. This is a
sad time; a lot of these people, if you just think of it, there's a whole
congregation of these people that she called that have passed away. I dont
know a lot ot them, mostley the ones from Alabama. Most all of you know
Leonard Lacy: l've known him as long as I've known anybody. He will be
missed; He was a very useful man. At my father and mother's funeral,
when they passed away, he was the leader of the singing, and I thought it
was the prettest singing that 1 ever heard. Anyway, we miss then all." He
then led song 101 and 566. Ginnie Ely then led 340 for the sick and
shut-ins as follows: Sarah Curry; Cleo Hawkins and Parks McNeal. Sarah
Barrie, Peggy Dillard, Virgil and Ruby Phillips, Noah and Margie Lacy and
Runie Glover Dollie Hudgins, Eva Reeves, Randall Hamilton, Dora Brake,
Pauline Spain. The memorial lesson was concluded with prayer by John
Bayer.
With time for one leader before dinner, Buell Cobb (AL) was called.
who prefaced his lesson on page 349, with the following: "I"d like to
preach instead of sing. I know that one of the many appeals of Sacred Harp
Singing is that we spend more of our time singing then preaching, but
do want to just say this one thing, and that is that for those of us


84


from the traditional areas that have been singing Sacred Harp before, the
Chicago Community seems like the gateway to the undiscovered territory.
This is not only the largest and the most vigorous of all the communities
that have taken up Sacred Harp recently, but it has also done a wonderful
job in shepherding other and smaller groups, and so for that, for the old
singers and the new singers everywhere, I salute you and thank you for
that." The chairman called for a special order of business and the class
then sang Happy Birthday to Charlie Ballard, Kathleen Kuiper Gary Gornau
and Master Thomas Jefferson Willard.

ALTAMONT FAIR (TRICOUNTY FAIR GROUNDS)
Near Albany New York
June 26, 27, 1993
A singing School and singing from the Denson book and Northern
Harmony were held on June 26 and 27 on the grounds of the Altamont Fair.
This is an annual singing held on the last Saturday and Sunday in June.
Because il is a part of the Old Songs Festival of traditional Music and
Dance, and time was at a premium, officers were not elected, nor were
leaders called. The Singing school on Saturday afternooon was taught by
Peter Amidon, who also led the singing, and who endorsed the submission
of these minutes. Each day, the singing began at 9:30 a.m. and conclued at
10:45 a.m. All lesson were from the Sacred Harp unless otherwise noted;
Songs used on Saturday as follows: 175, 49b; 107, 178, 273, 268, 569,
62, (Northern Harmony) ;191; "Grafton“' 31, 198, 62.
Songs used on Sunday, were: 48, 47, 454; 155, 268, 497; "Grafton"
146; 90 (Northern Harmony); 236, 200, 62. The song Grafton is out of
print, but has been circulated in New England by Larry Gordon.
Secretary, Linda Delfs.

CONNECTICUT CONVENTION
Middletown, Connecticut
July 11, 1993
The tenth Connecticut Sacred Harp Singing Convetniton was preceded on
Saturday, July 10, by the traditional all-day singing school taught by Hugh
McGraw, at the First Church of Christ Congregational in Middletown,
Connecticut. The singing school class was the largest to date and included
several new singers who had been introduced lo Sacred Harp singing at the
Old Songs Festival held near Albany, New York, a few weeks earlier, as
well as regulars of the Connecticut Corvention. An evening session of
singing from Northern Harmony was also part of the preliminary activites.
The Convention, also held at First Church, was opened on Sunday
morning by Susan Garber leading songs on pages 107 and 64. Hugh McGraw
gave the opening prayer. The class organized by electing the following

Richard Whatley expressed thanks to the Wilson Family and all others who
made the convention a success. Our resolution is one of thanksgiving and
praise. Thanks to a loving and gracious God who has sustained us, that we
might gather together in this holy place for one more meeting. Praise
because our meager songs fall far short of the true glory to which He is
entitled.
We especially wish to thank the Wilson family, without whose help and
generosity this convention could not have been held. Words can not express
the gratitude of everyone gathered here for their tireless efforts on behalf
of this convention. We would also like to recognize and thank the members
of the various organizing committees for their hard work, the ones who
prepared and brought the meals, and all the ones who came to lift their
voices in praise. Hay the Lord bless and keep you all till we meet again.
124

Charlene Wallace, Jeff Sheppard and Richard Whatley. The Finance
Committee expressed thanks for the collection. The convention closed in
the traditional way with officers leading song on page 62 and prayer by the

Leo Owen, Ruby Smith, Sudie Mae Burks, Rayburn Cathey, Monnie M. Ross,

Preston Crider, Noah Lacy, Leonard Lacy and Henry Johnson. Miss Craig

witnesses, open our eyes to behold, our ears to hear, our minds to recieve.
Amen. As we remember let us be aware that these have passed into that
life that is life indeed. Their lives serve as memorials to them. Now a cloud
of witnesses as a royal and very real presence, encompassinq us in such a
way as to afford real peace and comfort in times of distress and despair.
Entering into our deepest joys, and enriching each worship experience-
lending to each a new dimesnion. It has been my experience that, in times
of need, my loved ones are closer in the spiritual realm than they were in
the physical- Of the righteous dead the Bible speakes: their works do
follow them. We speak of then as dead, but, is anyone really dead who has
lived a good life?
Theodore Roosevelt, while mourning the untimely death of a young son,
concluded, Both life and death are a part of the same great advanture. When
we become Christians, we become resurrection people. A part of the body
of the resurrected Christ. We are told that we become the righteousness
of God through Christ. The resurrection of Christ is the most important
129


event the world has ever known. It is not just the hope, the assurance of
our own physical ressurrection. But, for the Christians, the important
thing is not that there will be this resurrection, but that we as Christians,
raised with Christ, can, and should, lead a resurrected life right here,
right now! The very thought should bring us to our knees, then lift us up to
joyful praise.
A. A Smith led song on page 285, for the closing of the memorial.

CLEBURNE COUNTY CONVENTION
Mars Hill Primitive Baptist Church
August 15, 1993
The Cleburne County Sacred Harp Singing Convention met at Mars Hill
Primitive Baptist Church, Cleburne County, Alabama, for the one hundred
and fourth session on Sunday. The session was called to order with the
class singing songs on pages 48, 59, 58, 47, 571, 569 and 163b. Cecil
Edwards led song on page 145b. The chaplain, Billy Joe Harris led the
morning prayer. The Introductory lesson was led by Elder Marshall Avery
leading song on pages 40, 159 and 108b. Officers who led were: Cecil
Edwards, 155; Walter Hamrick, 68, B. J. Harris, 119, 312b; Mary L.
Smith, 143. The class then organized by electing the following officers:
Chairman, Cecil Edwards; Vice Chairman, Walter Hamrick, Secretary,
133


Florence Smith; Chaplain, Billy Joe Harris; Arranging Committee, Hester
Edwards; Memorial Committee, Ruth Brown and Lois Bowman.
A business session was held at this time. The convention extends thanks to
Hester Edwards for having the old book containing the minutes restored for
future generations. Since no one had asked for the convention for another
year, it will come back to Hars Hill in 1994. The business session was
closed and Lovella Kerr led songs on pages 72b and 73. Harvey Austin,
101, 106.
RECESS.
The class was called together by Cecil Edwards leading song on page
404. Leaders: Margie Smith, 76b in memory of Carl Hughes, 77b; Sam
Sommers, 314, 485; Eula Johnson, 318, 460; Becky Fields and Lou
Cotney, 401, 405, 300, 212; Pearl Guier, 127, 63; Shelton Williamson,
166, 189; Eloise Avery, 448b, 480; Judy Mincey, 107, 34b. The
memorial was held at this time and Lois Bowman told of the many
memories she has of attending the convention in her early years and how
John and Millard McWhorter, John Newton Hamrick and others who worked
hard to keep the convention going. She recalled people coming from far and
near to attend the three day singing and spending the night at her parents
home. She recalled coming into the church yard and hearing the singing of
the large crowd and the beautiful treble voice of the late Ludie Lambert.
Many people she saw only once a year when they came to this Church for
this convention, are gone, as well as most all the Kerr. Those who are
passed and gone including families of the McWhorter, Hamricks, Kerrs,
Laminack and others, that we are singing for to day would wont us not be
sad but be glad. Ruth Brown read the following names for the memorial:
Noah and Leonard Lacy, Curtis McLendon, E. C. Bowen, Ella Mae Avery,
Preston Crider, Fred Howe, Clara Keener, Zera Tolleson and Henry
Johnson. Shelbie Sheppard led 278 and 66, B. M. Smith 348b, 354 for the
deceased and Lonnie Rogers led song on pages 45 and 298 for the sick and
shut-ins. Ruth Brown mentioned several of the sick and asked that we all do
something for some one who is worse off then we. The memorial was
closed with prayer by Chaplain, Billy Joe Harris.

Leaders: I. V. Cockrell, 192, 269. A memorial lesson was held at this time.
I ask that sach one here this morning have a prayer in your heart for me
and this memorial service. I feel priviledged to be here today with loving
and caring christian people, and to offer prayers for comfort to familes
and friends of those who have gone on before us. The following have gone
on a journey since we sang here a year ago. God grant that we may meet
together in the world above where troubles will cease and harmony will
abound. I want you to remember some thing about each one whose name
will call. The heartache and loneliness are here with us, but there is a
peace in knowing there is no more pain and suffering, also imagine we hear
the voice of the ones who proceded us. Do not stand by my grave and weep,
am not there, I do not sleep, I am a thousand winds that blow, I am a
diamond glint on snow, I am the sunlight on ripened grain, I am the gentle
autunm rain, when you awake in the morning, hush I am the swift uplifting
rush of quiet birds in circling flight, am the soft starshine at night. Do not
stand by my grave and cry l am not there. I did not die.
By saying these words my memory of each one gone on is not at the
grave yard, but in the notes that have been sung, the birds, breezes,
prayers and just evey day living brings them to mind each day of the year.
I would like to say one thing about Sacred Harp, precious memories flood
my soul and cause me to be proud of a heritage that continues to bless my
life. Face may dim as years come and go, but stoppinq again in the tracks
that have been made in the past is good medicine for the soul. Now for
Ephisisnas 5:19 Speak to one another with Psalms, Hymns and spiritual
songs- sing and made music in your heart to the Lord always giving thanks
to God the Father for every thing in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ". I
feel that each one who has preceded us will be singing with us in much
brighter tones than we can deliver on this earth. Edith Tate will led song
147


on page 46 for the following decease: Preston Crider, Henry Johnson, Billy
Galbreath, Belle Venie Woodard, Rosa McCoy, Bennie Keeton, Arthur Ward,
Leonard Lacy, Noah Lacy, E. C. Bowen. John Barfield, Curtis McLendon,
Priscilla Childers, Olmer Shipp, Arthur Calvert and Louie Battle. For the
sick and shut-ins we ask that our creator and our eternal God put his
loving arms around those who are unable to be with us because of sickness.
God cares about our sorrows and sickness and has promised to give
guidnance and comfort if we will but trust him. May the warmth of knowing
God flow over them and ease their pain and suffering. Let us ask ourselves
what he would have us do for the sick and disable, maybe send cards, have
a prayer on our lips or make a short phone call to some one. All would like
a kind word to express our care and build their hope and don't forget to say
I love you. Listen to the word as we sing Morning Prayer led by Velton
Chafin for the following Mavis and Leon Price, Flora Otwell, Lucile
Tolbert, Mamie Creel, Claudine Lancaster, Viva Barnes, Marie Guthrie,
Elsie Moon, Bertha Henson and Clarice Hollis. Velton Chafin led 411 and the
memorial was closed with prayer. Leaders: John Merritt, 235; Freeman
Wootten, 385, 385b; Robert Bonell, 569b, 457.

preceded us and gone on to the beautiful throne described by John in

the throne. We can be thankful for the heritage they have left us that
continues to bless our lives. A few years ago I really didn't know they sang
Sacred Harp except when we had homecoming at the church I grew up in.
thouht they came because they liked us. But l've found more fulminant in
my life since l've learned these praises are sung for and near and want
to thank each one of you for your personal interest in my learning to sing
Sacred Harp. Hay just single out one of our deceased, Preston Crider. He
was so good to lead me into and out of the songs which I tried to learn,
bless his soul, he was called to see the great white throne with a beautiful
rainbow around it. He death seem untimely to me, as l am sure he went so
suddenly. Life is so short we are here then gone like the beautiful snow
flakes they seldom fall here in the south, and like our lives, just disappear
so soon. they are like precious stones that we can hold a while, and then
they are gone. As I read the names, fix in your mind all the good thinqs
you can remember about each one, and pray that as we are here we can
show kindness and love that someone cares remember about us as life goes
on. The decease are Leonard Lacy, Noah Lacy, Preston Crider; Arthur

Singing was held on the second Sunday in October. This was the first
singing held in the newly constructed church building. The previous building
was destroyed by tornado since the 1992 singing. The singing was called to
order by CO-Chairmen Billy Thompson and Marty Wootten leading songs on
pages 31 and 31b. Rev. Clyde Allen led the morning prayer. Billy Thompson
and Marty Wootten led songs on pages 73 and 108. Leaders: Terry

Myra Dalton and Misty Dalton, 480; Lamar Smith, 36b, 76b; Bud Oliver,
345, 343b; David Ivey, 388; Joyce Walton, 192; Phil Summerlin, 73b;
99; Freeman and Jewel Wootten, 426, 317, 179.

week after the singing is held in order to be in the proper place. The
month of October and November must be received the week after the
singing is held. All Changes in addresses must be in by August
first. All Changes for the directory, must be in by November first.
